# Log — what was learned, when, in order

⛔ **APPEND-ONLY.** The only permitted edit to an existing entry is appending a
`⛔ retracted by L##` pointer. Never reword an entry to match what you now believe — the
whole point of this file is that it records what was believed at the time.

**The detailed log is the git history.** Every entry below names its commit; `git show <hash>`
gives the full reasoning, the numbers and the controls. This file is the index: one line per
finding so the arc is readable without `git log`, and so retractions are visible at a glance.

**Current knowledge is `FINDINGS.md`** — that file is rewritten freely and holds no history.
Nothing here should be read as current unless `FINDINGS.md` also says so.

---

Also: `lfprobe.py` now scores the LOAD arm, which was silently discarding 32 Indala reads against a loud HID tag — a loud-signal null we were already paying for | stress n=24, 3 tags |
| L61 | — | `f1b566a` | ⭐⭐⭐ **The wrong-credential path is closed: on the front the decoder now returns 110 frames and all 110 are correct.** C39's straddles are rejected by their SHAPE — where two adjacent bits differ, an integrator sitting across the boundary averages +1 and -1 and lands near zero, so the frame's weakest bit collapses relative to its average. Gate: reject when `mean|integ| >= 2048` AND `min|integ| * 8 < mean|integ|`. 21 of 21 straddles rejected, 110 of 114 true frames kept, 40 of 40 at rotation phases, and the back-side set completely unchanged at 68/51 (C48). ⛔ The obvious gate was wrong twice over: the winner-vs-straddle amplitude ratio separates BACKWARDS (1.30 good vs 1.64 wrong), and shape alone fails on the back where true frames are equally ragged — it would cost 61% of back-side reads. Both terms are needed because the two populations differ by an order of magnitude in level, not in shape (C49). ⚠ The amplitude term is absolute and so coupling-dependent, exactly what C43 warns about, so this does NOT retire the 60-92 keep-out; it is the second layer. ⚠ compare.py caught a 4x unit error in my own mirror of the gate — the firmware folds [1,2,1] without dividing — which is precisely the job that cross-check exists to do | 640 captures, both placements |

Every measurement in this project uses one credential, `a0000000e6bd0e92`, which has 19 ones — ODD — so the subcarrier inverts every frame and the non-inverting branch had never run. Synthesised `a0000000d2e6b438` (18 ones, EVEN) through the shipping decoder: indistinguishable from the reference at every amplitude, and the new gate rejects neither (C50). The white coin now carries that word on hardware. ⛔ Two self-confirming-test bugs caught on the way: the repeat-a-command idiom in NEXT §3 never ran anything (literal quotes, cu.py printed its help and it looked like ten reads), and my first parity harness put the expected word in the capture FILENAME, so `word in line` matched every line and the tally read '-2 wrong' | synthetic, 168 captures |

White coin (`a0000000d2e6b438`, EVEN parity, a different package) 20/20 at phase 20, **bit offset 22**, one capture each: the non-inverting branch works on real hardware, and the gate rejected nothing, so the 2048 threshold is not too high for a second package. ⛔ But offset 22 is one of the offsets the dead-band WRONG frames won at, while true frames on the bench tag won at 9/10/12. I had written that up as "offset 16, exactly half the 32-sample bit period" and called it the straddle signature. It is not: half a bit from 9 is 25, not 16, so the arithmetic never worked, and a correct read at 22 settles it. The offset belongs to the TAG's frame timing. ⇒ Corrected in four files. The gate itself is untouched because it tests shape and never looks at the offset (C51) | 40 hardware reads, 2 tags |

Only the 64 bits differ (preamble `0x4944544B`, "IDTK"). Every other null so far (HID FSK, EM410x ASK) differs in modulation, so the mixer and bit integrator never saw valid PSK1 carrying the wrong frame. Synthetic arm: **0 frames from 90 captures**, control 15/15 (C54). ⭐ `nulltest.py` now runs the whole procedure — amplitude bracket in the interferer's own band against a floor measured in that band, then the null, then the interferer's own reader as a BONUS that cannot fail the test — and refuses to score below 3x presence. The second Chameleon reads the copper coin 20/20 at the SAME sample phase 20, with only the bit offset moving 9 -> 10 (C56). ⛔ **My null band was wrong for the one null that mattered.** `nulltest.py` defaults to 500–20000 Hz, which covers envelope modulation but NOT a PSK1 subcarrier at fc/2 = 62500 Hz: IDTECK scored 5.5–6.3x where the ASK tags scored 16–19x, and that 6x is leakage. The amplitude bracket — the instrument F05 exists to insist on — was the WEAK evidence, and the interferer's own reader, which I had demoted to "a bonus that cannot fail the test", was the strong one: `lf idteck read` decodes PSK1 at fc/2, so it succeeding proves the tag was audible in the Indala decoder's own band and modulation (C57). Band table added to nulltest.py. ⚠ `lf pac read` scored 0/5 and 2/5 while its tag sat at 16x — another Chameleon LF decoder failing on a loud tag, alongside HID | 160 nulls, 2 units |
| L67 | — | `e4d71fa` | ⭐⭐ **§2's two offline re-opens, and they resolve opposite ways.** STACKING: on the front it gains **nothing** — 68.75% at every depth N=1..5, exactly the 22 good phases out of 32 — and in the dead band it actively *reinforces* the straddle, wrong frames converging from 9 distinct at N=1 to 4 at N=4 where every one of 20 is a repeat. More stacking defeats the agreement rule more thoroughly. The gate holds at 0 wrong throughout. On the back stacking is still worth 32% -> 72% ⇒ it earns its place only for the WRONG placement (C58). FRAME LOCK: the lag-0 correlation cited for C11–C13 is worthless — the EMPTY field scores 0.994 against the tag's 0.999 on the front and actually *higher* on the back (0.941 vs 0.912).
